# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

```plantuml
@startuml

title nexus Model

class EntanglementManager {
    activePairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    quantumComm : QuantumComm
    establishConnection() : EIntegerObject
    listActivePairs() : void
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    removePair(pairIndex : int) : void
    sendMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : void
}
class JsonHelper {
    escapeString(s : String) : String
    parseArray(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<EJavaObject>
    parseBool(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : boolean
    parseNull(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    parseNum(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : Number
    parseObj(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EMap
    parseObject(json : String) : EMap
    parseStr(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<String>
    parseValue(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    serializeMap(map : EMap) : String
    skipWhitespace(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : void
    toJsonString(s : String) : String
    toJsonValue(value : EJavaObject) : String
}
class MessageProtocol {
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    addMetadata(key : String, messageDict : EMap, value : EJavaObject) : void
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String, metadata : EMap) : String
    getMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : EJavaObject
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    removeMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : void
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}
class QuantumComm {
    entangledPairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    maxPairs : int
    random : Random
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    createEntangledPair() : DoubleArray2D
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String) : String
    measureState(pairIndex : int) : int
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    transmitMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : boolean
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}

EntanglementManager --> QuantumComm : quantumComm
MessageProtocol --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
QuantumComm --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
JsonHelper::skipWhitespace
Summary:
Advances a mutable character position past any whitespace characters in the given string.
Algorithm:
1. While pos points within s and s[pos] is whitespace, increment pos.
2. Stop at the first non-whitespace character or end of string.
Related Features:
- None
Related Classes: None
Input:
- s: source string to scan.
- pos: mutable integer-array-like cursor holding the current position.
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- s is not null.
- pos is not null and contains a valid mutable index reference.
- pos references a location within or at the end of s.
Post-condition:
- pos is updated to the first non-whitespace character position, or to s.length() if only whitespace remains.

# Operation Specifications
JsonHelper::skipWhitespace
Summary:
Advances a mutable character position past any whitespace characters in the given string.
Algorithm:
1. While pos points within s and s[pos] is whitespace, increment pos.
2. Stop at the first non-whitespace character or end of string.
Related Features:
- None
Related Classes: None
Input:
- s: source string to scan.
- pos: mutable integer-array-like cursor holding the current position.
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- s is not null.
- pos is not null and contains a valid mutable index reference.
- pos references a location within or at the end of s.
Post-condition:
- pos is updated to the first non-whitespace character position, or to s.length() if only whitespace remains.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
QuantumComm::transmitMessage
Summary:
Transmits a message through the entangled pair at the specified index.
Transmission succeeds most of the time but may fail with a small simulated random probability.
Invalid pair indexes are rejected by throwing IndexOutOfBoundsException.
Algorithm:
1. Validate pairIndex against entangledPairs bounds.
2. If invalid, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
3. Optionally validate or format the message payload as needed by the communication layer.
4. Generate a small random failure event.
5. Return true on success, false on simulated transmission failure.
Related Features:
- entangledPairs
- random
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, DoubleArray2D
Input:
- message: message payload to transmit.
- pairIndex: zero-based index of the entangled pair used for transmission.
Output:
- true if transmission succeeds.
- false if transmission fails due to simulated random failure.
Pre-condition:
- pairIndex must be within 0 <= pairIndex < entangledPairs.size().
- message is not null if the caller expects a meaningful transmission.
Post-condition:
- entangledPairs is unchanged.
- The returned boolean indicates delivery success.
- Invalid index triggers IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a basic message into a JSON-like string with recipient, message, and a newly generated
ISO instant timestamp. This is the lower-level formatter used by communication operations.
Algorithm:
1. Create a message object/map.
2. Store recipient and message.
3. Generate current timestamp in ISO instant style and store it.
4. Serialize the object to a JSON string via JsonHelper.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- recipient: destination identifier.
- message: message body text.
Output:
- JSON string containing recipient, message, and timestamp.
Pre-condition:
- recipient is not null.
- message is not null.
- jsonHelper is initialized.
Post-condition:
- The returned string represents a valid message object.
- Timestamp is generated at the time of formatting.

QuantumComm::receiveMessage
Summary:
Receives a message through the entangled pair at the specified index by measuring the pair
and returning a human-readable text result.
Algorithm:
1. Validate pairIndex against entangledPairs bounds.
2. If invalid, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
3. Measure the pair using measureState(pairIndex).
4. Construct and return the string:
   "Message received with measurement: <value>"
Related Features:
- entangledPairs
- random
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input:
- pairIndex: zero-based index of the entangled pair used for reception.
Output:
- String in the exact form:
  "Message received with measurement: <value>"
  where <value> is 0 or 1.
Pre-condition:
- pairIndex must be a valid index into entangledPairs.
Post-condition:
- entangledPairs is unchanged.
- The returned string reports the measured binary result.
- Invalid index triggers IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::measureState
Summary:
Measures the entangled pair at the specified index and returns a binary result.
The operation directly addresses the stored pair pool and rejects invalid indexes.
Algorithm:
1. Verify pairIndex is within entangledPairs bounds.
2. If invalid, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
3. Read the pair state and derive a binary measurement outcome.
4. Return 0 or 1.
Related Features:
- entangledPairs
- random
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input:
- pairIndex: zero-based index of an entangled pair.
- Valid range: 0 <= pairIndex < entangledPairs.size().
Output:
- Integer measurement result, either 0 or 1.
Pre-condition:
- entangledPairs is initialized and contains at least one pair for successful measurement.
- pairIndex must be within range.
Post-condition:
- No entangled pair is added or removed.
- The returned value is binary.
- Invalid index triggers IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON message string into a dictionary representation. Parsing rejects null,
invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null rawMessage.
2. Delegate to JsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
3. If parsing fails or the top-level value is not an object, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. Return the parsed map.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- rawMessage: JSON string expected to encode an object.
Output:
- Parsed message dictionary as EMap.
Pre-condition:
- rawMessage is not null.
Post-condition:
- The input string is unchanged.
- A valid object representation is returned on success.
- IllegalArgumentException is thrown for null, invalid JSON, or non-object input.

QuantumComm::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a parsed message dictionary. A valid message must contain recipient,
message, and timestamp, all non-null; metadata, if present, must be map-like.
Invalid messages cause IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null messageDict.
2. Confirm recipient, message, and timestamp fields exist and are non-null.
3. If metadata is present, confirm it is a map-like object.
4. Throw IllegalArgumentException on any violation.
5. Return true if all checks succeed.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- messageDict: parsed message dictionary to validate.
Output:
- true for a valid message dictionary.
- IllegalArgumentException for invalid input or invalid structure.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null for successful validation.
Post-condition:
- messageDict is not modified.
- Valid messages are accepted and reported as true.
- Invalid messages raise IllegalArgumentException.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
QuantumComm::transmitMessage
Summary:
Transmits a message through the entangled pair at the specified index.
Transmission succeeds most of the time but may fail with a small simulated random probability.
Invalid pair indexes are rejected by throwing IndexOutOfBoundsException.
Algorithm:
1. Validate pairIndex against entangledPairs bounds.
2. If invalid, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
3. Optionally validate or format the message payload as needed by the communication layer.
4. Generate a small random failure event.
5. Return true on success, false on simulated transmission failure.
Related Features:
- entangledPairs
- random
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, DoubleArray2D
Input:
- message: message payload to transmit.
- pairIndex: zero-based index of the entangled pair used for transmission.
Output:
- true if transmission succeeds.
- false if transmission fails due to simulated random failure.
Pre-condition:
- pairIndex must be within 0 <= pairIndex < entangledPairs.size().
- message is not null if the caller expects a meaningful transmission.
Post-condition:
- entangledPairs is unchanged.
- The returned boolean indicates delivery success.
- Invalid index triggers IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a basic message into a JSON-like string with recipient, message, and a newly generated
ISO instant timestamp. This is the lower-level formatter used by communication operations.
Algorithm:
1. Create a message object/map.
2. Store recipient and message.
3. Generate current timestamp in ISO instant style and store it.
4. Serialize the object to a JSON string via JsonHelper.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- recipient: destination identifier.
- message: message body text.
Output:
- JSON string containing recipient, message, and timestamp.
Pre-condition:
- recipient is not null.
- message is not null.
- jsonHelper is initialized.
Post-condition:
- The returned string represents a valid message object.
- Timestamp is generated at the time of formatting.

QuantumComm::receiveMessage
Summary:
Receives a message through the entangled pair at the specified index by measuring the pair
and returning a human-readable text result.
Algorithm:
1. Validate pairIndex against entangledPairs bounds.
2. If invalid, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
3. Measure the pair using measureState(pairIndex).
4. Construct and return the string:
   "Message received with measurement: <value>"
Related Features:
- entangledPairs
- random
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input:
- pairIndex: zero-based index of the entangled pair used for reception.
Output:
- String in the exact form:
  "Message received with measurement: <value>"
  where <value> is 0 or 1.
Pre-condition:
- pairIndex must be a valid index into entangledPairs.
Post-condition:
- entangledPairs is unchanged.
- The returned string reports the measured binary result.
- Invalid index triggers IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::measureState
Summary:
Measures the entangled pair at the specified index and returns a binary result.
The operation directly addresses the stored pair pool and rejects invalid indexes.
Algorithm:
1. Verify pairIndex is within entangledPairs bounds.
2. If invalid, throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
3. Read the pair state and derive a binary measurement outcome.
4. Return 0 or 1.
Related Features:
- entangledPairs
- random
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input:
- pairIndex: zero-based index of an entangled pair.
- Valid range: 0 <= pairIndex < entangledPairs.size().
Output:
- Integer measurement result, either 0 or 1.
Pre-condition:
- entangledPairs is initialized and contains at least one pair for successful measurement.
- pairIndex must be within range.
Post-condition:
- No entangled pair is added or removed.
- The returned value is binary.
- Invalid index triggers IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON message string into a dictionary representation. Parsing rejects null,
invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null rawMessage.
2. Delegate to JsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
3. If parsing fails or the top-level value is not an object, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. Return the parsed map.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- rawMessage: JSON string expected to encode an object.
Output:
- Parsed message dictionary as EMap.
Pre-condition:
- rawMessage is not null.
Post-condition:
- The input string is unchanged.
- A valid object representation is returned on success.
- IllegalArgumentException is thrown for null, invalid JSON, or non-object input.

QuantumComm::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a parsed message dictionary. A valid message must contain recipient,
message, and timestamp, all non-null; metadata, if present, must be map-like.
Invalid messages cause IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null messageDict.
2. Confirm recipient, message, and timestamp fields exist and are non-null.
3. If metadata is present, confirm it is a map-like object.
4. Throw IllegalArgumentException on any violation.
5. Return true if all checks succeed.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- messageDict: parsed message dictionary to validate.
Output:
- true for a valid message dictionary.
- IllegalArgumentException for invalid input or invalid structure.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null for successful validation.
Post-condition:
- messageDict is not modified.
- Valid messages are accepted and reported as true.
- Invalid messages raise IllegalArgumentException.

```text
  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EntanglementManager::sendMessage
Summary:
Sends a message through the active pair at the specified index. The manager is tolerant:
an invalid active index is ignored, and the operation does not throw for that case.
Algorithm:
1. Verify that pairIndex refers to an existing entry in activePairs.
2. If invalid, do nothing and return.
3. Otherwise delegate transmission to QuantumComm.transmitMessage(message, pairIndex).
4. Ignore the boolean result at the manager boundary; message delivery is handled by the lower layer.
Related Features:
- activePairs
- quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input:
- message: message payload to transmit.
- pairIndex: zero-based index of the active pair used for transmission.
- Valid pairIndex range: 0 <= pairIndex < activePairs.size().
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- quantumComm is initialized.
- activePairs is initialized.
- message is not null if lower-level transmission is expected to proceed.
Post-condition:
- If pairIndex is valid, QuantumComm is invoked to transmit the message.
- If pairIndex is invalid, activePairs and quantumComm state remain unchanged by this operation.

EntanglementManager::establishConnection
Summary:
Creates a new entangled pair through the underlying QuantumComm component and, if successful,
stores it in the manager’s active connection list. Returns the active-pair index for the new
connection. If the lower-level pair capacity has already been reached, the operation returns
no value.
Algorithm:
1. Request QuantumComm to create a new entangled pair.
2. If QuantumComm signals capacity exhaustion, return no value.
3. Otherwise append the returned pair to activePairs.
4. Return the index of the newly added pair in activePairs.
Related Features:
- activePairs
- quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D, EIntegerObject
Input:
- None.
Output:
- EIntegerObject representing the index of the newly established active connection.
- If no additional lower-level pair can be created, returns no value.
- Index range is a zero-based integer within activePairs.
Pre-condition:
- quantumComm is initialized.
- activePairs is initialized as a list.
- QuantumComm has not exceeded its maxPairs capacity, otherwise the operation cannot produce a new pair.
Post-condition:
- On success, activePairs contains one additional pair and the returned index identifies that pair.
- On capacity exhaustion, activePairs is unchanged and no value is returned.

EntanglementManager::removePair
Summary:
Removes an active connection at the specified index from the manager’s active connection list.
Invalid indexes are tolerated and leave the list unchanged.
Algorithm:
1. Verify that pairIndex refers to an existing entry in activePairs.
2. If invalid, do nothing and return.
3. Otherwise remove the pair at pairIndex from activePairs.
Related Features:
- activePairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input:
- pairIndex: zero-based index of the active pair to remove.
- Valid range: 0 <= pairIndex < activePairs.size().
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- activePairs is initialized.
Post-condition:
- If pairIndex is valid, the corresponding active pair is removed and the list size decreases by one.
- If pairIndex is invalid, activePairs remains unchanged.

EntanglementManager::receiveMessage
Summary:
Receives a message from the active pair at the specified index. The manager acts as a tolerant
facade: invalid active indexes or underlying communication failures result in no message.
Algorithm:
1. Verify that pairIndex refers to an existing entry in activePairs.
2. If the index is invalid, return no message.
3. Otherwise delegate to QuantumComm.receiveMessage(pairIndex).
4. If the lower-level receive succeeds, return its message.
5. If QuantumComm reports a communication error or failure, return no message.
Related Features:
- activePairs
- quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input:
- pairIndex: zero-based index of the active connection to receive from.
- Valid range: 0 <= pairIndex < activePairs.size().
Output:
- A human-readable message string on success, typically in the form:
  "Message received with measurement: <value>"
- No message if pairIndex is invalid or if communication fails.
Pre-condition:
- quantumComm is initialized.
- activePairs is initialized.
Post-condition:
- activePairs is unchanged.
- On success, a message string is returned.
- On invalid index or communication failure, no message is returned and no state changes occur.

EntanglementManager::listActivePairs
Summary:
Provides the current active connection list maintained by the manager.
This operation is intended as a read-only listing view and does not change state.
Algorithm:
1. Read the activePairs collection.
2. Expose or print the collection contents as the active connection list.
3. Do not modify any stored pair or manager state.
Related Features:
- activePairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No return value.
- The active connection list is accessed for display or inspection purposes.
Pre-condition:
- activePairs is initialized.
Post-condition:
- activePairs remains unchanged.
- The set/order of active connections is preserved.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.
```

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a message as a JSON-like string containing recipient, message, timestamp,
and optionally metadata. The timestamp is generated at formatting time using ISO instant style.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new message dictionary.
2. Set recipient and message fields from input parameters.
3. Generate current timestamp in ISO-8601 instant format and store it as timestamp.
4. If metadata is provided and non-empty, include it as a map-like object.
5. Serialize the dictionary to a JSON string using JsonHelper.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- recipient: destination identifier; must be representable as a string.
- message: message body text.
- metadata: optional protocol metadata map; may be null or empty depending on caller usage.
Output:
- A JSON string representing the message object.
- The output contains recipient, message, and timestamp; metadata is included when present.
Pre-condition:
- recipient is not null.
- message is not null.
- jsonHelper is initialized.
Post-condition:
- The returned string is a serialized object form of the message.
- The timestamp field reflects the time of formatting, not a later or cached time.

MessageProtocol::addMetadata
Summary:
Adds or updates a metadata entry in a message dictionary. If the metadata container does
not yet exist, it is created. This operation supports protocol-level annotations.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict does not contain a metadata object, create an empty map-like container for it.
2. Insert or replace the entry identified by key with the given value.
3. Preserve existing non-metadata message fields.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
- key: metadata field name to add or update.
- messageDict: message dictionary to modify.
- value: metadata value to store; may be any JSON-compatible object.
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- key is not null.
- messageDict is not null.
- messageDict is a map-like object that can contain a metadata dictionary.
Post-condition:
- The metadata container exists in messageDict after the call.
- The key is present with the supplied value.

MessageProtocol::removeMetadata
Summary:
Removes a metadata entry from a message dictionary. Removing a missing key is harmless
and does not alter the dictionary.
Algorithm:
1. Confirm that messageDict contains a metadata map.
2. If the map contains key, remove it.
3. If key is absent, do nothing.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- key: metadata key to remove.
- messageDict: message dictionary to modify.
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null.
- key is not null.
Post-condition:
- If key exists in metadata, it is removed.
- If key does not exist, messageDict remains unchanged.

MessageProtocol::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON message string into a message dictionary representation.
Invalid input is rejected with IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null rawMessage.
2. Use JsonHelper to parse the string into a map-like object.
3. Reject malformed JSON or any non-object top-level structure.
4. Return the parsed map.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- rawMessage: JSON-formatted string representing an object.
Output:
- Parsed EMap representation of the message object.
Pre-condition:
- rawMessage is not null.
- rawMessage is a valid JSON object string.
Post-condition:
- A map-like message dictionary is returned.
- The input string is not modified.
- IllegalArgumentException is thrown if the input is null, invalid JSON, or not a JSON object.

MessageProtocol::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a message dictionary. The operation returns true for a valid message and
throws IllegalArgumentException for invalid messages rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null messageDict.
2. Check that recipient, message, and timestamp fields exist and are non-null.
3. If metadata exists, verify it is map-like.
4. If any check fails, throw IllegalArgumentException.
5. Otherwise return true.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- messageDict: message dictionary to validate.
Output:
- true if the message satisfies all structural rules.
- IllegalArgumentException if the message is invalid.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null for successful validation.
Post-condition:
- The message dictionary is not modified.
- Valid messages yield true.
- Invalid messages cause IllegalArgumentException.

MessageProtocol::getMetadata
Summary:
Reads a metadata value from a message dictionary by key. If the key is missing,
the operation returns no value.
Algorithm:
1. Confirm that messageDict contains a metadata map.
2. Look up key within the metadata map.
3. If key exists, return its value; otherwise return no value.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
- key: metadata key to read.
- messageDict: message dictionary containing optional metadata.
Output:
- The metadata value associated with key if present.
- No value if the key is missing.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null.
- key is not null.
Post-condition:
- messageDict is unchanged.
- If the requested metadata exists, it is returned without modification.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a message as a JSON-like string containing recipient, message, timestamp,
and optionally metadata. The timestamp is generated at formatting time using ISO instant style.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new message dictionary.
2. Set recipient and message fields from input parameters.
3. Generate current timestamp in ISO-8601 instant format and store it as timestamp.
4. If metadata is provided and non-empty, include it as a map-like object.
5. Serialize the dictionary to a JSON string using JsonHelper.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- recipient: destination identifier; must be representable as a string.
- message: message body text.
- metadata: optional protocol metadata map; may be null or empty depending on caller usage.
Output:
- A JSON string representing the message object.
- The output contains recipient, message, and timestamp; metadata is included when present.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a message as a JSON-like string containing recipient, message, timestamp,
and optionally metadata. The timestamp is generated at formatting time using ISO instant style.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new message dictionary.
2. Set recipient and message fields from input parameters.
3. Generate current timestamp in ISO-8601 instant format and store it as timestamp.
4. If metadata is provided and non-empty, include it as a map-like object.
5. Serialize the dictionary to a JSON string using JsonHelper.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- recipient: destination identifier; must be representable as a string.
- message: message body text.
- metadata: optional protocol metadata map; may be null or empty depending on caller usage.
Output:
- A JSON string representing the message object.
- The output contains recipient, message, and timestamp; metadata is included when present.
Pre-condition:
- recipient is not null.
- message is not null.
- jsonHelper is initialized.
Post-condition:
- The returned string is a serialized object form of the message.
- The timestamp field reflects the time of formatting, not a later or cached time.

MessageProtocol::addMetadata
Summary:
Adds or updates a metadata entry in a message dictionary. If the metadata container does
not yet exist, it is created. This operation supports protocol-level annotations.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict does not contain a metadata object, create an empty map-like container for it.
2. Insert or replace the entry identified by key with the given value.
3. Preserve existing non-metadata message fields.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
- key: metadata field name to add or update.
- messageDict: message dictionary to modify.
- value: metadata value to store; may be any JSON-compatible object.
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- key is not null.
- messageDict is not null.
- messageDict is a map-like object that can contain a metadata dictionary.
Post-condition:
- The metadata container exists in messageDict after the call.
- The key is present with the supplied value.

MessageProtocol::removeMetadata
Summary:
Removes a metadata entry from a message dictionary. Removing a missing key is harmless
and does not alter the dictionary.
Algorithm:
1. Confirm that messageDict contains a metadata map.
2. If the map contains key, remove it.
3. If key is absent, do nothing.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- key: metadata key to remove.
- messageDict: message dictionary to modify.
Output:
- No return value.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null.
- key is not null.
Post-condition:
- If key exists in metadata, it is removed.
- If key does not exist, messageDict remains unchanged.

MessageProtocol::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON message string into a message dictionary representation.
Invalid input is rejected with IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null rawMessage.
2. Use JsonHelper to parse the string into a map-like object.
3. Reject malformed JSON or any non-object top-level structure.
4. Return the parsed map.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- rawMessage: JSON-formatted string representing an object.
Output:
- Parsed EMap representation of the message object.
Pre-condition:
- rawMessage is not null.
- rawMessage is a valid JSON object string.
Post-condition:
- A map-like message dictionary is returned.
- The input string is not modified.
- IllegalArgumentException is thrown if the input is null, invalid JSON, or not a JSON object.

MessageProtocol::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a message dictionary. The operation returns true for a valid message and
throws IllegalArgumentException for invalid messages rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. Reject null messageDict.
2. Check that recipient, message, and timestamp fields exist and are non-null.
3. If metadata exists, verify it is map-like.
4. If any check fails, throw IllegalArgumentException.
5. Otherwise return true.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
- messageDict: message dictionary to validate.
Output:
- true if the message satisfies all structural rules.
- IllegalArgumentException if the message is invalid.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null for successful validation.
Post-condition:
- The message dictionary is not modified.
- Valid messages yield true.
- Invalid messages cause IllegalArgumentException.

MessageProtocol::getMetadata
Summary:
Reads a metadata value from a message dictionary by key. If the key is missing,
the operation returns no value.
Algorithm:
1. Confirm that messageDict contains a metadata map.
2. Look up key within the metadata map.
3. If key exists, return its value; otherwise return no value.
Related Features:
- jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
- key: metadata key to read.
- messageDict: message dictionary containing optional metadata.
Output:
- The metadata value associated with key if present.
- No value if the key is missing.
Pre-condition:
- messageDict is not null.
- key is not null.
Post-condition:
- messageDict is unchanged.
- If the requested metadata exists, it is returned without modification.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information

  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.

  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * 4. Return the index of the newly added pair in activePairs.
   * Related Features:
   * - activePairs
   * - quantumComm
   * Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D, EIntegerObject
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * Output:
   * - EIntegerObject representing the index of the newly established active connection.
   * - If no additional lower-level pair can be created, returns no value.
   * - Index range is a zero-based integer within activePairs.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - quantumComm is initialized.
   * - activePairs is initialized as a list.
   * - QuantumComm has not exceeded its maxPairs capacity, otherwise the operation cannot produce a new pair.
   * Post-condition:
   * - On success, activePairs contains one additional pair and the returned index identifies that pair.
   * - On capacity exhaustion, activePairs is unchanged and no value is returned.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Integer establishConnection()
  {
    double[][] pair = getQuantumComm().createEntangledPair();
    if (pair == null)
    {
      return null;
    }
    getActivePairs().add(pair);
    return Integer.valueOf(getActivePairs().size() - 1);
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->

   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Verify that pairIndex refers to an existing entry in activePairs.
   * 2. If invalid, do nothing and return.

   * - activePairs
   * - quantumComm

   * - quantumComm is initialized.
   * - activePairs is initialized.
   * - message is not null if lower-level transmission is expected to proceed.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If pairIndex is valid, QuantumComm is invoked to transmit the message.
   * - If pairIndex is invalid, activePairs and quantumComm state remain unchanged by this operation.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void sendMessage(final String message, final int pairIndex)
  {
    if (pairIndex < 0 || pairIndex >= getActivePairs().size())
    {
      return;
    }
    getQuantumComm().transmitMessage(message, pairIndex);
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:

   * facade: invalid active indexes or underlying communication failures result in no message.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Verify that pairIndex refers to an existing entry in activePairs.
   * 2. If the index is invalid, return no message.
   * 3. Otherwise delegate to QuantumComm.receiveMessage(pairIndex).
   * 4. If the lower-level receive succeeds, return its message.
   * 5. If QuantumComm reports a communication error or failure, return no message.
   * Related Features:
   * - activePairs
   * - quantumComm

   *   "Message received with measurement: <value>"
   * - No message if pairIndex is invalid or if communication fails.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - quantumComm is initialized.
   * - activePairs is initialized.
   * Post-condition:
   * - activePairs is unchanged.
   * - On success, a message string is returned.
   * - On invalid index or communication failure, no message is returned and no state changes occur.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  }

   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

                pos[0]++;
    
```java
                switch (next) {
                    case '"':
                        sb.append('"');
                        break;

                        sb.append('\\');
                        break;
                    case 'n':
                        sb.append('\n');
                        break;
                    case 'r':
                        sb.append('\r');
                        break;
                    case 't':
                        sb.append('\t');
                        break;
                    case '/':
                        sb.append('/');
                        break;
                    default:
                        sb.append('\\');
                        sb.append(next);
                        break;
                }
            } else if (c == '"') {
                return sb.toString();

                sb.append(c);
            }
        }
```

```java
        if (pos[0] < s.length() && s.charAt(pos[0]) == ']') {

            return list;

     pos[0]++;
    
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
    
        while (pos[0] < s.length()) {
            char c = s.charAt(pos[0]);
            pos[0]++;
    
            if (c == '\\' && pos[0] < s.length()) {
                char next = s.charAt(pos[0]);
                pos[0]++;
    
                switch (next) {
                    case '"':
                        sb.append('"');
                        break;
                    case '\\':
                        sb.append('\\');
                        break;
                    case 'n':
                        sb.append('\n');
                        break;
                    case 'r':
                        sb.append('\r');
                        break;
                    case 't':
                        sb.append('\t');
                        break;
                    case '/':
                        sb.append('/');
                        break;
                    default:
                        sb.append('\\');
                        sb.append(next);
                        break;
                }
            } else if (c == '"') {
                return sb.toString();
            } else {
                sb.append(c);
            }
        }
    
        throw new IllegalArgumentException(
            "Invalid message format."
        );
```

```java
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

  public Map<String, Object> parseObj(final String s, final int[] pos)
  {
    pos[0]++;
    
        Map<String, Object> map =
            new LinkedHashMap<String, Object>();
    
        skipWhitespace(s, pos);
    
        if (pos[0] < s.length()
                && s.charAt(pos[0]) == '}') {
            pos[0]++;
            return map;
        }
    
        while (true) {
            skipWhitespace(s, pos);
    
            if (pos[0] >= s.length()
                    || s.charAt(pos[0]) != '"') {
                throw new IllegalArgumentException(
                    "Invalid message format."
                );
            }

            skipWhitespace(s, pos);

                    || s.charAt(pos[0]) != ':') {
                throw new IllegalArgumentException(
                    "Invalid message format."
                );
            }
    
            pos[0]++;
    
            Object value = parseValue(s, pos);
            map.put(key, value);
    
            skipWhitespace(s, pos);
    
            if (pos[0] < s.length()
                    && s.charAt(pos[0]) == ',') {
                pos[0]++;
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
    
        skipWhitespace(s, pos);
    
        if (pos[0] >= s.length()
                || s.charAt(pos[0]) != '}') {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException(
                "Invalid message format."
            );
        }
    
        pos[0]++;
        return map;
  }

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

  {
    pos[0]++;
        EList<Object> list = new BasicEList<>();  // 修改此处
        skipWhitespace(s, pos);
        if (pos[0] < s.length() && s.charAt(pos[0]) == ']') {
            pos[0]++;
            return list;
        }
        while (true) {
            list.add(parseValue(s, pos));
            skipWhitespace(s, pos);
            if (pos[0] < s.length() && s.charAt(pos[0]) == ',') {
                pos[0]++;
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        skipWhitespace(s, pos);
        if (pos[0] >= s.length() || s.charAt(pos[0]) != ']') {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid message format.");
        }
        pos[0]++;
        return list;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * @model posDataType="edu.nexus.IntArrary"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='manual'"
   * @generated
   */
  public EList<String> parseStr(final String s, final int[] pos)
  {
     pos[0]++;
    
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
    
        while (pos[0] < s.length()) {
            char c = s.charAt(pos[0]);
            pos[0]++;
    
            if (c == '\\' && pos[0] < s.length()) {
                char next = s.charAt(pos[0]);
                pos[0]++;
    
                switch (next) {
                    case '"':
                        sb.append('"');
                        break;
                    case '\\':
                        sb.append('\\');
                        break;
                    case 'n':
                        sb.append('\n');
                        break;
                    case 'r':
                        sb.append('\r');
                        break;
                    case 't':
                        sb.append('\t');
                        break;
                    case '/':
                        sb.append('/');
                        break;
                    default:
                        sb.append('\\');
                        sb.append(next);
                        break;
                }
            } else if (c == '"') {
                return sb.toString();
            } else {
                sb.append(c);
            }
        }
    
        throw new IllegalArgumentException(
            "Invalid message format."
        );
  }
```

# Task
You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
